The tests field carries none for an empty named test-path set; otherwise it carries the SHA-256 of that set after path separators and a leading current-directory prefix are normalized, duplicates are removed, and the paths are joined in sorted order. Thus one set in any order or spelling has one value, and a different set has a different value. The refresh check admits a refresh subject only with that verified record and a checks field of landing or none, so the reset path's tip peel never peels a commit record as a refresh; the read-only identity walk still steps through any commit whose non-record identity equals its parent, including an empty commit record. One producer writes every passed-checks record: it fixes the staged tree before the checks, refuses failing checks or one that leaves the index or worktree off that tree, and restores the index on every failed write; its line joins the message's final trailer block. The record-checks route refuses before the checks when commit hooks are off, the primary checkout holds the branch, the worktree is dirty (including untracked files), the commit-check declaration is absent or empty, the message carries the label, or the harness floor refuses; it reads the commit checks and derived test set from the lane's own declarations, with no default, and refuses before the checks when derivation refuses; a refusal leaves the branch and index unchanged. On the record-checks route the producer runs the commit checks then the derived files, records their digest in the unchanged grammar, and prints the set, listed evidence and reported gaps. A trailer without a checks field is not read. The reset path's tip peel removes only a verified refresh at the tip; the read-only identity walk walks across commits whose non-record identity equals their first parent's, peeling any disposable commit it meets to reach one those commits would otherwise hide, and ending at the first commit whose identity differs from its parent's or at its absent-parent and depth bounds - so a branch with no disposable commit under it still walks. Two commit classes are disposable and the read-only walk peels both: the station's refresh, admitted by the refresh check, and the machinery's own adjudication close, admitted by the mechanical-close check against exactly one parent and a no-renames changed-path stream lying wholly under the work registry. That stream must pair deleted specs from exactly one branch's active directory with additions of the same filenames under the completed directory, and only the move itself may create or destroy: an addition or deletion under the work registry that is not one half of a recognised move refuses the whole commit, since peeling it would measure a spec's injection or an archived row's destruction at the parent, where no reviewer saw it. Modifications ride along unrestricted, because a close's inbound relink rewrites other rows in place and only ever modifies. A mechanical close admits exactly one moved row; any other shape declines to peel toward more review. Its subject must equal the writer's composed subject exactly, so a public prefix and suffix with a forged middle is not a passed-checks record. The one-row clause refuses an empty close, which has no deletion, and every multi-row close; one deletion implies one source branch; and the subject composer takes a single id and refuses anything else, so no empty-id subject is ever composed. One property admits the two classes and it is not two rules: both are machine-authored end to end, and both move the tree without the lane having changed what it claims - the work registry is deliberately inside the identity because a spec's own claims are what a verdict is about, and the close carries those claims through unchanged. Every arm of both verifications fails toward review, since declining to peel measures at a later revision and can never ask for less - though not always for more: on the empty close the refused commit is identity-preserving, so the walk-through step reaches the same revision the peel would have, and the refusal guards the mechanical-close check's contract rather than any answer the gate gives. The two peels are not the same peel: the reset path's tip peel asks the refresh check directly rather than through the shared disposable test, so a mechanical close is never removed by the destructive reset path, which may only ever drop a refresh sitting literally on the tip. The governing identity composes the read-only walk and the tree identity for the branch tip or an explicit revision, including a round's reviewed commit and a trailer's carrier; the branch is given by its name, because the peel verifies a refresh commit against the branch it names and no refresh subject names HEAD. A review session's scope is recorded as full, narrow or final base..sha; the gating span admits a fresh full scope in either mode, or a final scope only where reviewer retention is on and the final reviewer is persisted; a narrow scope and a retained full scope never admit. The logged rounds join branch-scoped round files to coordinator session logs by train and ordinal, and each round's review phase is the joined log's declared phase rather than the one its own filename carries, so a file cannot claim a phase its session did not serve; a joined key whose logs declare more than one review phase yields no round. They retain only a gating span whose base equals the caller's claim base and whose reviewed commit the round file names. The round entries retain only rounds whose reviewed revision governs the identity under judgement and emit phase, ordinal and verdict entries through a verdict parser the caller supplies. One filing check applies: the first physical line is exactly the Reviewed full-sha opener (a CR ending is allowed; no leading blank or whitespace is), exactly one canonical VERDICT line, a finding count matching the file, and a change request naming a finding. A tree reads as already judged only for an entry carrying a verdict, and never with no base. The declared phases clamp the configured independent-review count to the available review phases. ### LLR LLR-262
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The coordinator's session log records each session's phase and its exact commit range (`# commits: before..after`), and records a REVIEW session's full or narrow scope (`# review-scope: base..sha`); session_range and parse_range read the range, review_logs yields the gating span with it, and range_paths lists the paths the range changed with renames split. scope_offenders names every changed path other than the REVIEW session's own verdict file, the round file named for the session's train, ordinal and logged phase under any reviewed sha and tag; record paths are not exempt. Right after a REVIEW session the loop asks scope_offenders of the range it just took and, on any offender, stops the run needing an operator with the paths named; a session log is append-only evidence, so every reader takes each log as the commit that ADDED it recorded it (log_history, review_logs), never at the branch tip, and the merge ladder's review-scope rung first refuses by name any commit in the lane that modified or deleted a session log, then asks the same function of every REVIEW session log the branch committed (review_scope_refusal) and refuses the merge by name, an unreadable log or range refusing too. After a REVIEW or CRITIQUE session, uncommitted changes not present before it, other than the coordinator's own session log, fail the draw through the existing failed-draw path - the route cooled and the same phase re-drawn - with those paths stashed under one entry named for the session, so the re-drawn session starts clean; when that stash fails the run stops needing an operator with the paths and the failure named, and nothing is re-drawn. read_verdict reads the verdict blob as committed at HEAD in both arms, so an uncommitted verdict is never routed on; session_body binds an adjudication's composed request beside its verdict before the loop calls it; after the call, run_iteration records the loop call's success from call_succeeded in plan, and adjudication_bookkeeping calls record_outcome against those bound kinds and uses its returned why as the completion obligation: a failed call is recorded failed, keeps that obligation and does not read its verdict. logged_rounds reads each round at the end of its session's recorded range, and only when that range changed the round file: a later commit rewriting the file changes nothing the gate counts, a file its session never committed is no round, and a key whose logs record different phases or ranges yields none. done_when.items reads a spec's Done-when section, through kitlib.registry.done_when_section, as items: a list item with its continuation lines, or a prose paragraph, across subsections. changes compares a claim-time text with a later one after stripping ticks, strikethrough and whitespace; a later item carries a claim-time one when it equals it or extends it with evidence in the form closed specs use - appended text opening with an evidence separator (a dash, an arrow, a bracket, a bar or a check mark) and carrying an evidence token (a capitalised completion word from the closed set LANDED, DONE, MET, VERIFIED, SHIPPED, PASSED, FIXED and COVERED, a path or test id, a backticked name, or a commit sha) - while any other appended text, after closing punctuation or not, is a change; and every claim-time item not carried is reported as changed and every later item carrying none as added. The claim warns by name on stderr for each claimed row with no Done-when item and still claims. When a review round is composed, the brief gains a block quoting each change between the spec under active/<train>/ at the lane's base and the lane's own copy. At merge, intake evaluates each claimed non-adjudication row. An unreadable claim refuses the merge-time mint, naming why; the merge itself stands and recovery is a trunk-side fix. For an absent claim, intake emits its stderr line that the Done-when check did not run and mints nothing. For a readable claim whose closed Done-when changed against trunk's pre-merge copy under active/<branch>, intake proceeds through the existing arms: when no verdict line or operator ruling in the merged tree binds the closed text's digest, it mints one brief-bearing done-when adjudication row, titled per row and branch so a re-run dedupes, with Adjudicates naming the closed work item and Context quoting each change; when a covering SUCCESSOR verdict carries Dispositions drafts, it mints the Done-when part's own drafts instead - the whole single-kind verdict or its one ## done-when section - and refuses repeated Dispositions inside that valid section or one supplying no draft. A sitting with no or repeated done-when section, or a DONE-WHEN line under another heading, is invalid, covers nothing, and leaves the uncovered arm to mint the goalposts row. review_logs reads the resolved final-reviewer mode once per call through reviewer_dials and passes it to every REVIEW log's gating_span, so all readers apply one mode.

The scope rule names every changed path other than the review session's own verdict file, the round file named for the session's train, ordinal and logged phase under any reviewed commit and tag; record paths are not exempt. Right after a review session the loop asks the scope rule of the range it just took and, on any offender, stops the run needing an operator with the paths named; a session log is append-only evidence, so every reader takes each log as the commit that added it recorded it, never at the branch tip, and the merge ladder's review-scope rung first refuses by name any commit in the lane that modified or deleted a session log, then asks the same rule of every review session log the branch committed and refuses the merge by name, an unreadable log or range refusing too. After a review or critique session, uncommitted changes not present before it, other than the coordinator's own session log, fail the draw through the existing failed-draw path - the route cooled and the same phase re-drawn - with those paths stashed under one entry named for the session, so the re-drawn session starts clean; when that stash fails the run stops needing an operator with the paths and the failure named, and nothing is re-drawn. - **Detail**
  - before: bound_items reads each current Done-when item that only ticks or evidences a claim-time item as that claim-time item; digest binds the work-item id and those items as sha256:<16 hex>. covering reads verdict lines under review records only through the one recorded-verdict reader: only a bound verdict whose route wrote the outcome recorded and which parses valid contributes its Done-when line with outcome CLARITY, BLESSED or SUCCESSOR and the digest; a failed, pending, unbound or other-class verdict covers nothing. An operator-confirmed or operator-overruled decision entry carrying that digest also covers; an unseen entry or retired reviewed key does not. claim_copy returns absent when its readable revision has no claimed specification for the work item, which releases every hold and produces neither a mint nor a brief; it returns unreadable when the revision or listed copy cannot be read, and unreadable_reason makes that a named hold at dispatch, hand close and merge and a mint or brief refusal. _copy_at gives both claim and current reads found, absent, or unreadable forms. blessing_owed owes when a readable current specification differs from a readable claim and has no cover; deleting its Done-when section reports every claimed item changed. An absent claim releases only for a row never claimed through that lane; an unreadable claim, unreadable current copy, or claimed row with no current active or closed specification holds naming why. spec_at returns path, text and why, and lane_hold compares a lane's claim at its base with its current or closed copy at the requested revision; the terminal homes are docs/archive/work/complete, partial or cancelled, or the legacy docs/work equivalent. For the commit being made, a staged entry for a claimed specification other than a lone addition is a change, and it is a close when the same staged change adds that specification in a terminal home; a lone addition is a new claim and is not listed. A claimed specification's lone modification is not a change when its staged bytes are exactly the inbound link redirect caused by another specification's move from active into a terminal home that the same staged change carries; any other modification, a real edit beside the redirect, an unreadable blob, or bytes that are not UTF-8 is a change. On the claim's own branch, only a close is judged against the lane's integration base; on the declared trunk, the branch the policy home names, in any worktree, every listed change is judged against the commit's parent; elsewhere, only a close is judged against the current commit. The trunk is read once whenever a claim-copy change is listed; an undeclared trunk holds every listed change naming the declaration, while a delta listing no claim-copy change never reads it. An unreadable staged change set or branch identity holds naming the failed read, while outside a version-controlled work tree nothing is judged. Every path the staged-change reader classifies and every claim or current copy it looks up is read from a lossless, unquoted record listing, so a path that would otherwise be quoted is judged like any other. At the commit-time hold, each listed change is compared with HEAD, which is the commit's parent only for an ordinary commit. The routes outside the supported commit routes and the two fail-closed shapes within the supported routes are stated once, as residue, in the shipped hook's `done-when-blessed` comment.

The claim read and the current read each answer found, absent or unreadable. A blessing is owed when a readable current specification differs from a readable claim and has no cover; deleting its Done-when section reports every claimed item changed. An absent claim releases only for a row never claimed through that lane; an unreadable claim, unreadable current copy, or claimed row with no current active or closed specification holds naming why. The trunk is read once whenever a claim-copy change is listed; an undeclared trunk holds every listed change naming the declaration, while a delta listing no claim-copy change never reads it. An unreadable staged change set or branch identity holds naming the failed read, while outside a version-controlled work tree nothing is judged. Every path the staged-change reader classifies and every claim or current copy it looks up is read from a lossless, unquoted record listing, so a path that would otherwise be quoted is judged like any other. ### LLR LLR-339
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The integration branch is named by one declaration in the policy home, read by every reader that needs the trunk. A checkout on that branch is the trunk whether it is the primary checkout or a linked worktree. The trunk is never inferred from the branch a checkout is on; an absent, non-text or empty declaration, or one that names no local branch, is refused naming the declaration by every reader that judges or writes against the trunk. With an undeclared trunk, path-triggered step selection reads the change as unknown and runs every opted-in step, the readability report skips each declared measure naming the declaration, and the worker preflight's terminal-row check keeps its guard. A scaffold writes the branch it is on into the declaration only in a policy configuration created in the same scaffold run, never an adopter's existing file; in an unborn repository it writes the branch git will create, and when the destination has no repository or is detached, the template's value stays and the scaffold names the line to set. A lane's integration base is its merge base with the declared trunk, in a single checkout as in a linked worktree.
  - after: The integration branch is named by one declaration in the policy home, read by every reader that needs the trunk. A checkout on that branch is the trunk whether it is the primary checkout or a linked worktree. The trunk is never inferred from the branch a checkout is on; an absent, non-text or empty declaration, or one that names no local branch, is refused naming the declaration by every reader that judges or writes against the trunk. With an undeclared trunk, path-triggered step selection reads the change as unknown and runs every opted-in step, the readability report skips each declared measure naming the declaration, and the worker preflight's terminal-row check keeps its guard. A scaffold writes the branch it is on into the declaration only in a policy configuration created in the same scaffold run, never an adopter's existing file; in an unborn repository it writes the branch git will create, and when the destination has no repository or is detached, the template's value stays and the scaffold names the line to set. A lane's integration base is its merge base with the declared trunk, in a single checkout as in a linked worktree; with no merge base, as on a history sharing no commit with the trunk, it is the checkout's own tip, and a checkout with no commit has none.

## SR-157 — Spine and work-registry rules fail the harness verdict

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (the tracked work-item graph the loop derives its next work from is kept coherent). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.

- **SR-Refs**: SR-157
- **Title**: An interface whose reach through the rows constraining it and the cases naming it holds no executable test case is reported
- **Detail**: An interface's reach is the design rows and system requirements whose constrains cell names it, the design rows and requirements a test case naming it verifies, and every test case naming it or verifying one of those rows, a constraining design row's cases including those of the design rows below it in the requirement tree, as a constraining requirement's include those of the design rows decomposing it. Each live interface whose reach holds no test case the harness runs, an observation case being one it does not run, is named on one warning line, and those whose reach holds observation cases alone are named on a line of their own. A count line gives the interfaces reached through a constraining design row, through a constraining requirement, through a naming test case alone, by observation alone, and not at all. ## SR-167 — Performance-budget breaches fail their hard-gated rows

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall fail the checks it runs exactly when an in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row breaches — its measured metric worse than its absolute Budget, or regressed beyond its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline — while warn-gated rows and out-of-tier rows only warn, and a metric with no measurement this run is a reported skip, never a silent pass.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a pass — a breach on a row the project chose to hard-gate must fail the run, not ride as a tracked number) and SN-012 (the perf layer is opt-in; a repo declaring no PB rows pays nothing, while one that does gets the same honest-gate discipline as every other check). This row is the requirement home for the perf verdict itself: SR-006 states the step plan and the missing-tool refusal, SR-015 the PB back-link invariant, and SR-033 only the warn-tier, never-fails side, so the exit contract the perf step already implements, decomposes and proves had no requirement stating it. One row for BOTH breach arms deliberately — absolute and regression are one delivered exit contract, the SR-157 one-verdict shape, and a regression-only framing is the symptom, not the boundary. THE REQUIREMENT NAMES NO ARTIFACT: the comparator is not on the declared delivered-artifact list — it is the harness's internal perf step — so the SR states the harness-verdict observable and the comparator is named only in acceptance as the current carrier, the SR-157/SR-158 pattern. Vacuous in THIS repository — no performance-budgets registry exists here and the perf layer is declared absent — but the template ships the layer, and a shipped layer owes a stated, tested obligation even where locally unused. One act is deliberately still owed: LLR-014.sr_refs and TC-014.verifies are NOT re-pointed here — both rows are Accepted, so amending their cells overrides acceptance, which i… [455 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-014
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Compares measured metrics (docs/test/perf-metrics.json) to declared budgets and the committed baseline (docs/test/perf-baseline.json) and fails beyond tolerance at the row's block/warn level; --update-baseline is the reviewed acceptance path for an intended regression. The step is wired into the harness at DevStg-Impl, per its declared gates in docs/stack.ini.
  - after: Compares the measured metrics to the declared budgets and the committed baseline and fails beyond tolerance at the row's block or warn level; the baseline update is the reviewed acceptance path for an intended regression. Each in-tier budget that fails or warns prints a line naming its status, its budget id, its metric and the reason. A fail-level budget with no measurement this run prints a WARN line naming it and marked as a skip, rather than passing in silence, and is counted among the summary's skips, not its warnings; a warn-level budget with no measurement prints no line of its own and is counted among the skips. The run closes on an OK or FAIL summary line counting the in-tier budgets, the failures, the warnings and the skips; a project with no budget rows prints one OK line saying so, and budgets with no metrics file print one SKIP line naming where the metrics were expected. A comparison exits 1 only when an in-tier budget fails, and 0 for every other outcome, warnings and skips included. The baseline update compares nothing: it merges the current metrics into the baseline and exits 0, and with no metrics to write from it prints that it cannot update the baseline, naming where the metrics were expected, and exits 1. A run that reaches a comparison writes the performance report: one table row per in-tier budget giving its status, budget id, metric, measured value, baseline, delta against the baseline, budget, direction and gate, then a findings list naming the reason for each failing and each warning budget; the no-budgets, no-metrics and baseline-update runs write no report. The step is wired into the harness at DevStg-Impl, per its declared gates in the stack profile.
_adjudicated link — goes to a sitting_
- **Constrains**
  - before: (empty)
  - after: IF-004;IF-147;IF-167

### TC TC-014
- **Expected**
  - before: Satisfies the acceptance folded into LLR-014, under both its parents: SR-167's perf-budget breach verdict (the absolute and regression arms this case drives) and SR-006's harness step plan that runs it, including the stage the step is wired at
  - after: With no budget rows one OK line, and with no metrics file one SKIP line naming where the metrics were expected, each exiting 0. A fail-level breach prints a FAIL line naming the budget, its metric and the reason and exits 1; a warn-level breach prints a WARN line and exits 0; a regression within tolerance passes and one beyond fails; the out-of-tier budget fails only at its own tier; a non-numeric metric fails at fail level and warns at warn level, naming the value. The unmeasured fail-level budget prints a WARN line marked as a skip and the warn-level one no line, and the summary, the last line, counts both as skips and no warning. The report holds one row per in-tier budget giving its status, id, metric, measured value, baseline, delta, budget, direction and gate, and a finding naming the reason for the failing and the warning budget, and the out-of-tier budget has no row. The baseline update merges the metrics into the baseline and exits 0; with no metrics it prints that it cannot update the baseline, naming where the metrics were expected, and exits 1, writing no baseline. The no-budgets, no-metrics and baseline-update runs write no report. The harness's DevStg-Impl run over the fail-level breach fails at the budget comparator's step, which the harness lists as a process-layer step. This satisfies SR-167's breach verdict and SR-006's step plan through LLR-014.
- **Method**
  - before: Run the check-perf suite; a within-tolerance metric passes and a regression fails.
  - after: Run the performance-budget comparator over a fresh scaffold: with no budget rows; with budgets and no metrics file; a fail-level and a warn-level budget past their absolute budget; a regression within and beyond its tolerance against the baseline; a fail-level budget outside the run tier, at the run tier and at its own; a non-numeric metric on a fail-level and on a warn-level budget; a fail-level and a warn-level budget with no measurement; a fail-level breach with a baseline, a warn-level breach and an out-of-tier budget, reading the report; and the baseline update with metrics and with none, reading the report after each run that compares nothing. Run the harness at DevStg-Impl over a fail-level breach, and list its steps.

## SR-168 — The state view shows current progress and next work

> **Requirement.** The delivered generator set shall produce one state view from which a reviewer reads the adopted repository's completeness, its requirement decomposition, its planned and in-flight work, and any declared hold on that work, without consulting a second surface.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-023's FIRST conjunct — a reviewer sees the project's progress from one dashboard-like file — with SN-010 (what the reviewer reads there is the generated truth, never a hand-kept summary of it). ONE DECISION PER ROW: the integrity of a generated artifact (SR-070) and what that artifact must SHOW are two obligations that fail independently. This row is the progress half; SR-169 is the connection half, along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws. Deliberately NOT folded back into SR-070: a state view that regenerates deterministically, offline and drift-checkably while omitting the work in flight satisfies that row completely and this one not at all. Deliberately NOT folded into SR-169 either: a complete progress view can carry no seam graph whatsoever — the state this repository was in before its interface registry existed — so the two are separately observable and separately buildable. `phase` inherits the parent's 1 rather than tracking its children (which span 1 through 4), because a phase records WHEN a piece shipped, and child-later-than-parent is the ordinary case.

## SR-170 — Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.

> **Rationale.** ONE DECISION PER ROW: a shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. This row holds the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract alone; ordering-and-all-or-nothing is SR-173's and identity allocation SR-174's, each with its own children. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). The serial LANDING (SR-156) and the rule that shared authority surfaces regenerate only there are two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already ruled for the identity mint — a work branch never mints a work-item id — and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.


### LLR LLR-137
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Compiles docs/log.d/*.md into docs/log.md via plan_artifacts.append_log_summary in (git add-time, filename) order - all fragments validated first (name shape, ## heading, reserved section headings refused, an adding commit required - on HEAD, or on MERGE_HEAD during the integrator's merge composition), relative links rebased from docs/log.d/ to docs/, fragments deleted after append, all-or-nothing on any validation error. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op.
  - after: Compiles the session log fragments into the log, appending each through the log's shared appender, in the order of the commits that added them, oldest first, with the file name breaking a tie. Every fragment is validated first: its name shape, an opening second-level heading, a reserved log section heading refused, and an adding commit required, on the checked-out history or, while the integrator composes a merge, on the branch being merged. Relative links are rebased from the fragment directory to the log's, fragments are deleted after the append, and the compile is all-or-nothing on any validation error, a refused compile leaving the log unchanged and every fragment in place. Dotfiles and the directory's own readme are not fragments and are never removed. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op. ## SR-173 — Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial result

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall regenerate the shared derived artifacts in declared dependency order, stopping at the first failure and committing no partially regenerated set.

> **Rationale.** THE TRANSACTIONALITY HALF of three obligations that fail independently: ordering and all-or-nothing are one decision about a regeneration run, and they are not the same decision as who is allowed to write (SR-170) or how identity is allocated (SR-174). A regeneration that runs in the right order and still commits half a set satisfies neither, which is what makes them separable. Realizes SN-008 - a first failure that stops loudly beats a half-regenerated set that reads as complete - and SN-010.
THE GUARANTEE IS "COMMITS NO PARTIAL SET" RATHER THAN "LEAVES NONE BEHIND", and the narrower wording is the one the delivered behaviour supports: the regeneration step touches git NOT AT ALL, so on a first failure it exits nonzero having run no later step, with HEAD unmoved and nothing committed - and it DELIBERATELY LEAVES the already-passing steps' output dirty in the working tree, because the design assigns the commit to the caller (LLR-142's "never commits; the caller owns the commit"). The residue is not tolerated, it is REQUIRED: TC-170's own evidence test asserts `git status --porcelain` is NON-EMPTY after the failure, so an implementation that cleaned up after itself would fail the test that holds this row. The transactional rollback a reader might otherwise expect exists, but it belongs to the WRAPPING callers (the integrator and intake each reset the branch to its last work commit on failure) and is their obligation, not this row's - which is why stating it here would credit t… [60 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-137 — ADDED since the snapshot
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-137
- **SR-Refs**: SR-170;SR-173
- **Title**: Serial trunk step (log compile + regen)
- **Detail**: Compiles the session log fragments into the log, appending each through the log's shared appender, in the order of the commits that added them, oldest first, with the file name breaking a tie. Every fragment is validated first: its name shape, an opening second-level heading, a reserved log section heading refused, and an adding commit required, on the checked-out history or, while the integrator composes a merge, on the branch being merged. Relative links are rebased from the fragment directory to the log's, fragments are deleted after the append, and the compile is all-or-nothing on any validation error, a refused compile leaving the log unchanged and every fragment in place. Dotfiles and the directory's own readme are not fragments and are never removed. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op. Asked for the compile, the regeneration, or neither, which runs both with the compile first since it can move the log the generators read, the step runs exactly those; a dry run prints what each selected operation would do and writes nothing. The step exits 0 when every selected operation finished or found nothing to do, and 1 on any failure, naming the offending fragment or generator on its error stream; a failed compile stops the step before the regeneration.
- **Rationale**: One serial actor per §5.5.
- **TestRefs**: (see TC-130)
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Phase**: 4
- **Constrains**: IF-081;IF-155;IF-156;IF-168

## SR-178 — Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported

> **Requirement.** The kit shall report any recorded artifact whose text has moved away from the
copy recording its acceptance - stakeholder needs included - regardless of any
Status movement.

> **Rationale.** THE DRIFT half, separable from SR-140 in the direction that matters: a record that exists and rides its acceptance commit, with nothing ever comparing live text against it, satisfies SR-140 completely and leaves every later amendment invisible. That is not a hypothetical - it is precisely the failure the git-history derivation had, and the reason a baseline on disk was owed at all.
REGARDLESS OF STATUS MOVEMENT is the load-bearing clause, not a qualifier. The amendment machinery watches the Status flip, and the sanctioned staged-amendment path deliberately does not flip - so a rule keyed off the flip returns nothing on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. Reporting against the recorded copy is the only reading that survives that, because the copy is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file and a walk over the live file can never be one.
THE STAKEHOLDER-NEED RIDER is in the normative text rather than left to the acceptance because the need tier is the one a sitting most wants to hold and the one the amendment-watching rules were last to reach: each rule walks the tiers it is given, so without the clause the need tier is left off the walk and no rule reaches it. Needs are compared row by row under the same accepted and traced split as every other tier.
WHAT COUNTS AS MOVEMENT is one definition with one home, shared with the amend-without-flip guard: the id column and the Status cell are excluded STRUCTURALLY (the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in woul… [817 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-359
- **Detail**
  - before: A row's accepted-text digest is the identity of the text a sitting judges on it, formed from its nonempty accepted cells and its nonempty adjudicated links (LLR-484), excluding its id and Status: carrier representation and an absent versus empty cell give the same identity, a moved accepted cell or adjudicated link changes it, and a moved trace cell no adjudicator judges does not. An amendment brief renders one judged-text identity line for each row it shows for judgement. Reservation writes those identities in the binding, and rewriting the binding as recorded or failed preserves them. A held CLARITY re-acceptance is actable only when the accepted binding's identity equals the committed row's digest, and an absent, malformed or unequal identity yields a refusal before launch naming the row, an unequal one naming both digests; a held acceptance claim is excepted only under a CLARITY verdict whose binding records the row's equal digest. The contract covers registries, recorded copies and bindings the kit's own routes write; a hand-edited recorded copy, act ledger or binding is outside it.
  - after: A row's accepted-text digest is the identity of the text a sitting judges on it, formed from its nonempty accepted cells and its nonempty adjudicated links (LLR-484), excluding its id and Status: carrier representation and an absent versus empty cell give the same identity, a moved accepted cell or adjudicated link changes it, and a moved trace cell no adjudicator judges does not. An amendment brief renders one judged-text identity line for each row it shows for judgement, together as one block. A composed brief of any kind whose text reads as identity lines other than exactly the ones its composer wrote, as one block naming each row once, is refused at composition, naming the row each differing line names and where it sits: a line of that shape the composer did not write, whether in a shown row's cell, in a later section of a combined sitting or anywhere else in its text; a line the composer wrote that no longer reads as one, its template having moved the identity slot off the start of a line; or lines that are not one block naming each row once. A brief whose identity block ends its text is refused too, so a binding records exactly the identity lines the composer wrote. Reservation writes the identities of that one block in the binding, and rewriting the binding as recorded or failed preserves them; it refuses, naming each line and writing no binding, a brief carrying a line of that shape outside one block of distinct rows, or any such line when it requests no amendment, so text added to a composed brief after its composition can neither add an identity nor replace one. A held CLARITY re-acceptance is actable only when the accepted binding's identity equals the committed row's digest, and an absent, malformed or unequal identity yields a refusal before launch naming the row, an unequal one naming both digests; a held acceptance claim is excepted only under a CLARITY verdict whose binding records the row's equal digest. The contract covers registries, recorded copies and bindings the kit's own routes write; a hand-edited recorded copy, act ledger or binding is outside it.
- **Rationale**
  - before: A verdict about one text cannot authorize a later text unless the text the verdict saw has a stable identity carried from brief reservation through the later act. The adjudicated links are in the identity because an act naming the row records them with its accepted text (LLR-245), so a link moved after the verdict would otherwise be recorded under a verdict that never judged it. Comparing the committed identity with the accepted binding keeps a changed, absent or malformed identity from being read as the judged row.
  - after: A verdict about one text cannot authorize a later text unless the text the verdict saw has a stable identity carried from brief reservation through the later act. The adjudicated links are in the identity because an act naming the row records them with its accepted text (LLR-245), so a link moved after the verdict would otherwise be recorded under a verdict that never judged it. Comparing the committed identity with the accepted binding keeps a changed, absent or malformed identity from being read as the judged row. Only the composer's own identity lines may reach the binding because a judged cell is shown verbatim and may span lines, and a brief carries other text that is not the composer's (a later section, a spec's context, a template, and any section appended to it before reservation): a line of the identity's shape there would add an identity no shown text has or, after the genuine line, replace one, and a held act could then take a row whose text the verdict never judged. Composition refuses such a brief, keeping judged text verbatim and closing every source of its text at one point; reservation, which reads the brief as a route hands it, binds only one block of distinct rows, so a line added after composition is refused too, a block ending a brief is refused so an appended line cannot join it, and a composer's line its template hides from the reader is refused so an appended line cannot stand in its place.

Judge a held acceptance claim under a CLARITY verdict binding its equal digest, and one whose adjudicated link moved after the verdict.

Compose an amendment brief whose shown row's moved cell, a first-acceptance brief whose awaiting row's cell, and a combined sitting whose later first-acceptance section's row cell holds a line of the judged-text identity's shape, each in turn naming that row, another row the brief shows and a row it does not show; a combined sitting whose Done-when section's context holds one naming the amendment section's row; a combined sitting whose own template holds one; every other routed brief kind with one of its slots holding one, and each again without it; an amendment brief whose template ends with its identity block; one whose template moves its identity slot off the start of a line, and the same brief with an identity line appended before a sitting is reserved from it; and one whose composer's identity lines are split apart or name a row twice. Compose the amendment, first-acceptance and combined briefs again without the line, read their judged-text lines and reserve a sitting from each. Reserve a sitting from a composed combined brief with a section appended holding such a line, from a brief requesting no amendment with an appended section holding one, and from a brief whose one block names a row twice.

## SR-208 — Held-rung changes are refused before they land

> **Requirement.** Where the declared acceptance level holds a snapshotted registry's rung for an operator, the delivered harness shall refuse, before it lands on trunk, both a lane's acceptance claim that is not a CLARITY re-acceptance bound to the row's judged text and a marked autonomous commit outside a lane that moves a held status or makes a held acceptance claim.

> **Rationale.** The acceptance level names the rungs an operator still accepts, and an automated acceptance of the spine's own rows is already refused. The frame, assumption, surrogate and component registries carry status cells of their own that no automated path was stopped from changing, because the one setting that would stop it defaults to allowing. A policy that declares a rung operator-held without refusing the automated path is a claim, not a control. A lane can carry an acceptance into trunk even when no automated writer made its commit, and an autonomous commit outside a lane can make the same change; protecting only selected writers leaves the held state dependent on who delivered it. Binding the permitted re-acceptance to its judged text prevents a verdict about one text authorizing another, while leaving an unmarked reviewed trunk acceptance to its operator authority.

The contract covers registries, recorded copies and bindings the kit's own routes write; a hand-edited recorded copy, act ledger or binding is outside it.
- **Rationale**
  - before: A verdict about one text cannot authorize a later text unless the text the verdict saw has a stable identity carried from brief reservation through the later act. The adjudicated links are in the identity because an act naming the row records them with its accepted text (LLR-245), so a link moved after the verdict would otherwise be recorded under a verdict that never judged it. Comparing the committed identity with the accepted binding keeps a changed, absent or malformed identity from being read as the judged row.
  - after: A verdict about one text cannot authorize a later text unless the text the verdict saw has a stable identity carried from brief reservation through the later act. The adjudicated links are in the identity because an act naming the row records them with its accepted text (LLR-245), so a link moved after the verdict would otherwise be recorded under a verdict that never judged it. Comparing the committed identity with the accepted binding keeps a changed, absent or malformed identity from being read as the judged row. Only the composer's own identity lines may reach the binding because a judged cell is shown verbatim and may span lines, and a brief carries other text that is not the composer's (a later section, a spec's context, a template, and any section appended to it before reservation): a line of the identity's shape there would add an identity no shown text has or, after the genuine line, replace one, and a held act could then take a row whose text the verdict never judged. Composition refuses such a brief, keeping judged text verbatim and closing every source of its text at one point; reservation, which reads the brief as a route hands it, binds only one block of distinct rows, so a line added after composition is refused too, a block ending a brief is refused so an appended line cannot join it, and a composer's line its template hides from the reader is refused so an appended line cannot stand in its place.

### TC TC-398
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Expected**
  - before: The digest has the sha256 shape and is unchanged by the id, the Status, the unjudged trace cell, empty against absent and the carrier, and changes for the accepted cell and the adjudicated link. Each brief states one judged-text line per row shown for judgement, carrying that row's digest as its registry holds it, the link-only row's covering its link. The binding records the prompt's identities, reading its requested kinds ignores them, and both rewrites keep them. The held act is owed only under the equal binding; the moved cell, the moved link and the accepted-cells-only binding of a row carrying a link are unequal identities, and each refuses it naming the row, the digest its binding records and the committed row's digest; the missing and the malformed identity each refuse it naming the row; and the act turn refuses before launch. The claim is excepted under the equal binding and not when the link moved.
  - after: The digest has the sha256 shape and is unchanged by the id, the Status, the unjudged trace cell, empty against absent and the carrier, and changes for the accepted cell and the adjudicated link. Each brief states one judged-text line per row shown for judgement, carrying that row's digest as its registry holds it, the link-only row's covering its link. The binding records the prompt's identities, reading its requested kinds ignores them, and both rewrites keep them. The held act is owed only under the equal binding; the moved cell, the moved link and the accepted-cells-only binding of a row carrying a link are unequal identities, and each refuses it naming the row, the digest its binding records and the committed row's digest; the missing and the malformed identity each refuse it naming the row; and the act turn refuses before launch. The claim is excepted under the equal binding and not when the link moved. Each brief holding a line of the identity's shape its composer did not write is refused at composition, naming the row that line names and the line's place; the brief whose identity block ends its text, the one whose moved slot hides its composer's line, refused naming that row so no sitting is reserved from it with a line appended, and the one whose composer's lines are split apart or name a row twice are each refused; each routed brief kind composes without the line; without the line, each brief's judged-text lines are exactly its composer's, as one block, none for the first-acceptance brief, and its binding records exactly those. Each reservation from a brief carrying a line outside one block of distinct rows, or any such line while requesting no amendment, is refused naming that line, and writes no binding.
- **Method**
  - before: Digest a requirement row, then the same row with its id, its Status and a trace cell no adjudicator judges changed, with an empty cell against an absent one, and on its other carrier; then with an accepted cell moved, and with an adjudicated link moved. Compose an amendment brief over committed rows whose accepted cells moved, and one over a row whose adjudicated link alone moved, and read their judged-text lines. Reserve a sitting from a composed prompt, round-trip and read its binding, and rewrite it as recorded and as failed. Derive the held act of a CLARITY verdict whose binding equals the committed row, one whose row's accepted cell moved after it, one whose row's adjudicated link moved after it, one whose binding was written over the row's accepted cells alone, one with no identity and one with a malformed identity cell, reading each refusal for the row and, where the identities are unequal, for the digest the binding records and the committed row's digest; and launch the act turn over one whose text moved. Judge a held acceptance claim under a CLARITY verdict binding its equal digest, and one whose adjudicated link moved after the verdict.
  - after: Digest a requirement row, then the same row with its id, its Status and a trace cell no adjudicator judges changed, with an empty cell against an absent one, and on its other carrier; then with an accepted cell moved, and with an adjudicated link moved. Compose an amendment brief over committed rows whose accepted cells moved, and one over a row whose adjudicated link alone moved, and read their judged-text lines. Reserve a sitting from a composed prompt, round-trip and read its binding, and rewrite it as recorded and as failed. Derive the held act of a CLARITY verdict whose binding equals the committed row, one whose row's accepted cell moved after it, one whose row's adjudicated link moved after it, one whose binding was written over the row's accepted cells alone, one with no identity and one with a malformed identity cell, reading each refusal for the row and, where the identities are unequal, for the digest the binding records and the committed row's digest; and launch the act turn over one whose text moved. Judge a held acceptance claim under a CLARITY verdict binding its equal digest, and one whose adjudicated link moved after the verdict. Compose an amendment brief whose shown row's moved cell, a first-acceptance brief whose awaiting row's cell, and a combined sitting whose later first-acceptance section's row cell holds a line of the judged-text identity's shape, each in turn naming that row, another row the brief shows and a row it does not show; a combined sitting whose Done-when section's context holds one naming the amendment section's row; a combined sitting whose own template holds one; every other routed brief kind with one of its slots holding one, and each again without it; an amendment brief whose template ends with its identity block; one whose template moves its identity slot off the start of a line, and the same brief with an identity line appended before a sitting is reserved from it; and one whose composer's identity lines are split apart or name a row twice. Compose the amendment, first-acceptance and combined briefs again without the line, read their judged-text lines and reserve a sitting from each. Reserve a sitting from a composed combined brief with a section appended holding such a line, from a brief requesting no amendment with an appended section holding one, and from a brief whose one block names a row twice.

## SR-209 — Every commit the loop makes carries a checked provenance trailer

> **Requirement.** When a commit is made from a process the unattended loop started, or is merged in a lane the loop owns from the point the loop took that lane over, the delivered commit floor shall refuse it unless it carries a valid provenance trailer naming the loop and its session.

> **Rationale.** A status change on an operator-held rung can be told apart from an operator's only if every loop-made commit says it is one. Without a floor that holds every loop commit to it, a trailer could appear on only some of the loop's commits and a missing one would prove nothing. Where a commit is made, the condition is who made it, not when: a person committing in another checkout while the loop runs is not the loop. Where a lane merges, the floor sees commits, not processes, so it reads whose lane it is from the commits and judges every commit of a loop lane, a person's included: there an unmarked commit the loop made cannot be told from a person's, and a person's commit belongs in the person's own lane. The commits made before the loop took a lane over are exempt, since nothing then could have marked them. Refusing the unmarked commit at the floor makes the mark total, which is what lets a later check treat its absence as meaningful. The floor where a commit is made is local and a determined bypass defeats it, which is why the merge re-checks a loop lane and the history check stands behind both.

### LLR LLR-306
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: ENTRY. coordinator_adjudicate runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. It also refuses an unusable route, invalid account, registry error, or unreadable or undecodable brief through that interface; its sign-in reading identifies the adjudicator entry’s account. As its last pre-launch checks it creates the verdict path's missing parent directories and then the path itself, empty and exclusively, and creates its requested-kinds binding beside it exclusively, so an existing or concurrently reserved path or binding, or one whose parent cannot be created, also refuses; its exclusive binding is made through the shared bind_pending request binder; a pre-launch refusal removes only the reservation and binding this call created and leaves any directory it created. Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an ADJUDICATE call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit, and prints the IF-285 reading. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.
  - after: ENTRY. The coordinator's adjudication entry point runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. It also refuses an unusable route, invalid account, registry error, or unreadable or undecodable brief through that interface; its sign-in reading identifies the adjudicator entry’s account. As its last pre-launch checks it creates the verdict path's missing parent directories and then the path itself, empty and exclusively, and creates its requested-kinds binding beside it exclusively, so an existing or concurrently reserved path or binding, or one whose parent cannot be created, also refuses; its exclusive binding is made through the shared request binder; a pre-launch refusal removes only the reservation and binding this call created and leaves any directory it created. Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an adjudication call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit. It prints the call's retention, a fresh session, a retained session minted or one resumed, and the call's exit, then, for a successful call, the verdict it wrote with its validity or that it wrote none, and, for a failed call, that its verdict is not read. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it prints the call's retention and exit, then the acts still owed after the call and the review records the call changed, or that the act is complete; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.
- **Rationale**
  - before: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness, while IF-283, IF-284 and IF-285 each own the command contract they expose; copying those contracts here was rejected because two homes for the same route or exit condition would drift.
  - after: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness. The command's interfaces state its form, its default route and its exit codes, and this row states the behaviour a caller observes across them: the refusals, the recorded outcome and the reading it prints.

## SR-215 — At a checkpoint, an unjudged, expired or triggered observation test is queued once for re-judging

> **Requirement.** When a work item merges, a release is prepared or a stage gate is checked, the delivered harness shall file one re-judge work item per due observation case: one with no result, one whose result expired, or one whose declared trigger fires after its work-item floor; with no declared trigger, a change to its declared inputs makes it due, subject to that floor. It keeps at most one open item per case, and reports each observation case that cites no written pass criteria as a warning.

> **Rationale.** Judgements cost time and model calls and vary across sessions. Written pass criteria fixed before the first judgement make the pass reviewable. The closed-work floor and the declared trigger are cost limits the operator directed (the PERFORMANCE lens): no change or checkpoint makes an accepted judgement due again within the configured number of closed work items of its latest record, and a declared trigger replaces input changes with the change or checkpoint it names, so a judgement can stand on changes it never judged until a qualifying change or checkpoint meets the floor or its result expires. First judgement and expiry keep missing or old evidence from standing indefinitely.

### LLR LLR-265
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: `intake.py sweep --before <sha> --after <sha> --branch <lane> --merged WI-###[;WI-###]` runs intake_after_merge over the range with an outcomes map holding exactly the named rows: merged_outcomes reads each one's outcome from the one terminal folder holding its spec (complete, partial or cancelled), so the early-close dispositions, the clean-close spot checks and the drafts a merged adjudication row names in its Dispositions section are minted for those rows and no others, beside the range's diff triggers (the amendment and first-acceptance rows) and the merge checkpoint's re-judge drafts at `--after`. `--merged` judges one merge, so _merged_shape_refusal refuses it without both range ends, when the two name one commit, and without `--branch`. The branch is the claim identity the Done-when check reads: a named row whose claim is under `active/<branch>/` at `--before` is checked against it, and one whose claim is not there gets one stderr line naming the row, the branch and why the check did not run; `--branch` names the mint commit either way, and a range or history sweep hands the check no claim identity. A named row in no terminal folder, or in more than one, refuses the whole sweep by name and mints nothing. `--merged` and `--with-terminal` are mutually exclusive; the range shape without either still judges no close, and the bare sweep still judges every close on file. Idempotent by the mint's exact-title dedup, like every sweep.
  - after: The intake's sweep command, given a range's two ends, the lane branch and the rows the merge closed, runs the merge slot's intake over the range with an outcomes map holding exactly the named rows: each one's outcome is read from the one terminal folder holding its spec (complete, partial or cancelled), so the early-close dispositions, the clean-close spot checks and the drafts a merged adjudication row names in its Dispositions section are minted for those rows and no others, beside the range's diff triggers (the amendment and first-acceptance rows) and the merge checkpoint's re-judge drafts at the range's later end. Naming merged rows judges one merge, so the sweep refuses it without both range ends, when the two name one commit, and without the branch. The branch is the claim identity the Done-when check reads: a named row whose claim is in the branch's active claim folder at the range's earlier end is checked against it, and one whose claim is not there gets one standard-error line naming the row, the branch and why the check did not run; the branch names the mint commit either way, and a range or history sweep hands the check no claim identity. A named row in no terminal folder, or in more than one, refuses the whole sweep by name and mints nothing. Naming merged rows and asking for the terminal scan are mutually exclusive; the range shape without either still judges no close, and the bare sweep still judges every close on file. Idempotent by the mint's exact-title dedup, like every sweep. A sweep that mints exits 0 naming how many rows it minted, as does one with nothing to mint, saying so; a refusal of a named-row sweep, for its range, its branch or a row's terminal folder, mints nothing and exits 1 with its reason on standard error.

## SR-220 — Overlapping queued work is consolidated through one judgement per queue state

> **Requirement.** While the queued work items include a set that overlaps, the delivered loop content shall hand that set to a single judgement, at most once for any one state of the queued work items and never while another judgement is in progress, queued over an item of the set, or queued to run before it, whose outcome takes effect only as a recorded restructuring in which each absorbed item is closed in a terminal state naming its one successor, its scope text unchanged.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that no human curate what comes next; its acceptance states only that the next work derives from the tracked work-item graph, which a stranger reading it would derive as work selection alone. This row states what that promise needs once the loop also mints work: rows minted one finding at a time overlap, and without this row either a human merges them, which is the curation the need rules out, or the loop builds the same job more than once. Two lenses shape it. UNATTENDED-OPS: an overlap is a standing state, so a judgement fired on it re-asks the same question on every idle tick unless the judged state is remembered past the judging row's close, an unbounded retry that pages nobody. PERFORMANCE: each judgement is a strong-tier session, so asking once per queue state is the operating-cost bound rather than an optimisation. The restructuring is recorded rather than done by deletion so that an absorbed item's scope and its successor stay readable after the act. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name the loop keeping its own queue free of duplicated work; until it does, this row is derived and says so. Choosing the next work stays SR-148's, and reporting an overlap as a warning stays SR-157's.

## SR-222 — Every model session is recorded in one usage record, whichever provider CLI serves it

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall record every model session it launches, whichever provider command-line runner serves it, in one usage record: token usage under a published usage vocabulary pinned to a named revision, input counted inclusive of cached input, fresh input derived by one formula, the runner's raw usage kept verbatim, the route's provider filled from its roster row for every routed call, the runner named, the vocabulary's gen_ai.provider.name holding the runner's default provider for claude and codex and empty for any other runner, with runner reconfiguration to another provider not detected, and the context occupancy of the session's latest request kept apart from its billed tokens; for a runner other than claude, codex and opencode, the runner name is left empty, and so is every count or raw usage the loop cannot read from the runner's output.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that the operator can configure several model families and route work among them per job and capability level; it does not name a usage record, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for measurements that cannot be compared, or are unavailable, being read as a reliable total: routing among families is a cost and quality choice, and the choice is blind when one family's calls record their usage and another's do not, or when the runners disagree on what "input tokens" means (two count cached input apart from input, one counts it inside), so one column would hold two incompatible numbers. The consistency lens listens for readers disagreeing after a one-sided change: when each launching role keeps its own launch and logging path, a usage fix lands in one path and not the others. A published vocabulary avoids inventing a log format for a problem that is not novel; because it has no tagged release, the record pins the exact revision it follows. The raw usage is kept verbatim so a mapping later found wrong is re-derived rather than lost. Occupancy is kept apart from billed tokens because they answer different questions: billed tokens are summed over every request the session made, while how full a context is depends on the latest request alone, and a session reset that read the first as the second would fire on sessions that are nowhere near full. A runner who… [376 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

## SR-223 — A guarded session receives the guardrails payload vendored for its model

> **Requirement.** Where a repository vendors a guardrails payload for a model-name substring, the delivered loop content shall inject that payload, in place of the default guardrails core, into a guarded session whose model name contains that substring.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that models be selected per job and capability level; it does not name what a session on a given model is told, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A guardrails posture written for one model is ritual noise to a stronger one and too little for a weaker one, and in an unattended run nobody watches a session drift: one core for every guarded model is the silent degrade that lens listens for. Selecting by a substring of the model name reuses the matcher the guardrails policy already applies, so one grammar decides both whether a session is guarded and what it is given, and the repository's own file names carry the mapping. A per-model key in the policy dial was the alternative, and it lost because a model name in shared configuration is the name that rots when models turn over. Fed back to the need: SN-026's acceptance could name a per-model posture; until it does, this row is derived and says so.
