  - before: guardrails_core(root, model) lists the vendored core.<substring>.md payloads beside docs/guardrails/core.md, orders their substrings longest first and then by name, and reads the first one that guardrails_apply matches against the model name, reading core.md when none matches or no model is named. The KIT CORE block is extracted from the chosen file as from the core, and an unreadable chosen file returns None, which compose_session_prompt already reports once and runs past. Whether a session is guarded stays compose_session_prompt's call on the policy, made before the payload is read.
  - after: The guardrails payload reader lists the payloads a repository vendors beside its default guardrails core, each a core file named for a model-name substring, orders their substrings longest first and then by name, and reads the first one the guardrails policy's own matcher matches against the model name, reading the default core when none matches or no model is named. The block between the KIT CORE markers is extracted from the chosen file as from the default core, and an unreadable chosen file reads as no core, which the session prompt's composition reports once and runs past without one. Whether a session is guarded stays that composition's call on the policy, made before the payload is read.

## SR-225 — A delegated run closes with a record of the calls it made, where the declared dial asks for one

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall keep the operator's verdict on each delegated decision coupled to the work that acts on it and each delegated run to its record: whatever the declared decision-recording dial, reporting without refusing a record entry that omits a required disclosure field or leaves one blank, treating each run's record as the listing the operator reads, in which an entry with no verdict is not yet seen, and refusing an overrule unless its transition change files or amends open work that cites the entry; and, where that dial asks for a record, refusing to integrate a closing lane whose record is absent, naming where it belongs.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-029 asks that a run released to automation get as far as it honestly can, and that an acceptance it makes on a released tier leave a record naming who made it; it does not name the other calls a delegated run makes on the operator's behalf, the ones too settled to hold the run for and not settled enough to be history, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A call nobody is told about is the failure that lens listens for: it pages nobody, and a run that seems to pass does so partly because nothing looked at what it chose. Required fields and a close refusal make that record durable rather than an instruction that can be omitted. The obligation is keyed to a dial because how much of the operator's reading a run may claim is the operator's to set; it ships off, so a repository owes nothing until its owner asks. Every close owes the record, a partial close included, because every delegated run closes with one; a lane the machinery closed with no session present is refused too, and that refusal is a hold for a person to write the record rather than a strand. A malformed entry is reported rather than refused, because the record is there and readable and a refusal would hold finished work for a reporting defect. A decision is an already selected direction, so the operator either confirms it or overrules it; acceptance would falsely imply a new selection. A closed verdict makes … [1282 more chars — read the registry row]

## SR-227 — Where the retention dial is on, adjudication routes retain sessions and reset them only when safe

> **Requirement.** Where the declared adjudicator retention dial is above zero, the delivered adjudication content shall manage each retained-class adjudication consistently whether the coordinator or loop starts it: resume the route's active, unheld session in its model runner's resume form; mint and record a session id where the route has no active session; wait a bounded time where its active session is held, then run unretained and state why; record each call like any other; drain the session when its latest request's occupancy reaches the dial or the inputs it judges under change; retire it only when no work it has a stake in is pending, except that at the first keep after a blackout window it retires a session without a last use or last used before that window ended with reason blackout even when its chain is pending; retire it at once when a call fails except that an authentication failure records the failed call and leaves its session record unchanged; let no two calls use one retained session at once; write the retention state only whole, by one writer at a time; make any keep-warm call one bounded turn that never blocks the scheduler; compose both routes' retention request once; and, before retaining a family that uses a dedicated CLI home, read any long-lived credential at each launch from the location a declared environment setting names, keep every verbatim occurrence out of logs, records and commits, and refuse an absent or unreadable credential before launch, naming setup without… [336 more chars — read the registry row]

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that a configured agent implement toward the vision with no human curating what comes next; it does not name retaining a session, so this obligation arrives through three lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for an operating-cost risk left unassessed: a run that spins a fresh adjudicator for every small work item reloads the spine on each call, and the usage record makes that cost visible, while resuming a session by id uses the provider's prompt cache. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a retained session that has filled, judges under changed rules, or is started in a home whose sign-in cannot be read degrades every later judgement with nothing paging anyone; a coordinator route that bypasses the retained operation repeats that cost and loses the state the loop preserves. One composition keeps both routes under the same drain and lease rules. The integrity lens listens for a durable artifact updated in place without an all-or-nothing guarantee and for a claim nothing can reclaim: two calls resuming one transcript at once corrupt it, so one call at a time holds a session, under a hold that expires when its holder is gone. Refusing an unreadable or absent sign-in before that hold prevents a failed retained launch from changing state or escaping into a person's default home. Retiring only when no work the session has a stake in is pending keeps a review, rework and … [503 more chars — read the registry row]

The probe environment is the prepared environment with the launch credential withheld. OUTPUT. The launch step redacts each verbatim occurrence of the credential value from physical output lines and the captured stream before the result, usage, logs or records consume it; escaped, encoded and line-split representations are outside that bound. For a qualified OpenCode account, the environment starts from its declared platform and network-transport base plus the kit-owned names, then the route pairs, the account pairs and the home, never the ambient environment; an OpenCode account declaring its auth-content variable is refused. No registry reread, fallback or automatic sign-in occurs. The governing-template step receives the request's role: for a review it returns the once-resolved reviewer template text the brief is built from, and for an adjudication it keeps the retained-class identity; the shared keep step passes that role. The governing identity of a retained set holding amendment, first-acceptance or combined also covers the act brief, by its wired override text when present and otherwise by its shipped text.

### LLR LLR-306
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: ENTRY. coordinator_adjudicate runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. It also refuses an unusable route, invalid account, registry error, or unreadable or undecodable brief through that interface; its sign-in reading identifies the adjudicator entry’s account. As its last pre-launch checks it creates the verdict path's missing parent directories and then the path itself, empty and exclusively, and creates its requested-kinds binding beside it exclusively, so an existing or concurrently reserved path or binding, or one whose parent cannot be created, also refuses; its exclusive binding is made through the shared bind_pending request binder; a pre-launch refusal removes only the reservation and binding this call created and leaves any directory it created. Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an ADJUDICATE call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit, and prints the IF-285 reading. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.
  - after: ENTRY. The coordinator's adjudication entry point runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. It also refuses an unusable route, invalid account, registry error, or unreadable or undecodable brief through that interface; its sign-in reading identifies the adjudicator entry’s account. As its last pre-launch checks it creates the verdict path's missing parent directories and then the path itself, empty and exclusively, and creates its requested-kinds binding beside it exclusively, so an existing or concurrently reserved path or binding, or one whose parent cannot be created, also refuses; its exclusive binding is made through the shared request binder; a pre-launch refusal removes only the reservation and binding this call created and leaves any directory it created. Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an adjudication call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit. It prints the call's retention, a fresh session, a retained session minted or one resumed, and the call's exit, then, for a successful call, the verdict it wrote with its validity or that it wrote none, and, for a failed call, that its verdict is not read. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it prints the call's retention and exit, then the acts still owed after the call and the review records the call changed, or that the act is complete; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.
- **Rationale**
  - before: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness, while IF-283, IF-284 and IF-285 each own the command contract they expose; copying those contracts here was rejected because two homes for the same route or exit condition would drift.
  - after: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness. The command's interfaces state its form, its default route and its exit codes, and this row states the behaviour a caller observes across them: the refusals, the recorded outcome and the reading it prints.

## SR-229 — Coordinator claim admission respects a blackout pause

> **Requirement.** Where a blackout window is armed, the delivered work-item admission shall refuse a new claim inside that window on every route at any context-guard dial; outside a blackout window, where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, it admits a coordinator claim only from the current lease holder while that holder's context drain is not latched, the drain latching once the holder's context occupancy reaches the declared threshold and holding across compaction and resume until the operator records a clear or a release, the holder records a hand-back naming its handoff, or a relaunched successor takes the lease.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that a declared pause stop claiming and drain what is already in flight, and that recovery never double-assign work; it does not name the coordinating session's own context, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a coordinator whose context has crossed its declared limit keeps claiming after compaction has degraded the context it needs to close the work it already holds, and nothing pages anyone. The integrity lens listens for a recovery path written only for the unattended run when the same corruption is reachable attended, and for a claim nothing can reclaim once its holder is gone: a retained adjudicator session already drains on occupancy while the attended coordinator does not, and a second or missing holder leaves claim authority ambiguous, so one lease names the coordinator and only the operator's recorded release frees a lease whose holder has gone. A latched single-holder admission boundary was chosen over a one-time event observation because every claim must remain protected when an event is missed or the transcript later reports a lower reading. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name the coordinating session's own capacity as a declared reason to stop claiming and drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-301 — ADDED since the snapshot
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-301
- **SR-Refs**: SR-230;SR-229
- **Title**: Coordinator relaunch request and exit acquisition
- **Detail**: The session-prompt reading extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. The relaunch request refuses inside a blackout window and otherwise accepts only the lease holder and an existing handoff, then writes a same-session request atomically. Inside a blackout window the hook's session-end handling cancels a pending request and records blackout; otherwise the session-end handling acts only for a true exit by that holder in its declared repository, rejects a foreign request, and atomically renames one request to acquire it and holds the store lock from that acquisition until the launch is confirmed or the request restored, so restoration never waits on the lock. It writes a fresh successor token into the lease and the handoff prompt to a file, invokes the platform launcher detached in the declared root with that file and token, and on any failure before launch confirmation restores the request and removes the successor token and the prompt file. The launch selects the Windows or POSIX launcher, waits through its grace period and confirms only a still-running launcher or one that exits zero. Each launcher enters the declared root, resolves one interpreter by running its virtual-environment candidate followed by the candidates from its development setup's ordinary runtime search, and takes the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it exits nonzero with guidance when none resolves, runs the g… [854 more chars — read the registry row]
- **Rationale**: The request must be owned, durable and consumed once, because a close-out that launches twice can create two coordinators and a failed launch must leave the handoff recoverable.
- **TestRefs**: TC-318
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Phase**: 6
- **Constrains**: IF-278;IF-279;IF-280;IF-281

### TC TC-318 — ADDED since the snapshot
- **TC-ID**: TC-318
- **Verifies**: SR-230;LLR-301;IF-278;IF-279;IF-280;IF-281
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Drive relaunch requests and session-end payloads with a stub launcher, and the guard's command line. Assert holder and handoff validation, clear and resume refusal, single atomic acquisition under concurrent exit handlers, restoration after a launch failure of any kind, including while another handler waits on the store lock, foreign-request refusal, detached launch arguments, both platform launchers and the successor-token variable they export being the one the guard takes the lease by, the command line's take, request-relaunch, handback, release and clear with its exit codes - success, a refusal with its reason on standard error, a usage error, and a hook call that always succeeds - and the dry run. Assert blackout window-check refuses, no request is written inside it, and SessionEnd cancels a pending holder request.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: An owned request starts one successor only at a true exit and remains recoverable on launch failure. During blackout no relaunch request or successor is created, and a pending request is cancelled as blackout.
- **Automated**: Yes

## SR-230 — Coordinator successor at session exit outside a blackout pause

> **Requirement.** Where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, when the coordinator lease holder that has requested a relaunch from its handoff ends its session outside an armed blackout window, the delivered coordination support shall start exactly one successor session from that handoff; inside the window it cancels the pending request and leave resumption to the operator.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that claiming drain what is already in flight and that a crash at any lifecycle boundary recover without double-assignment; it does not name a coordinating session handing over to its successor, so this obligation arrives through the integrity lens rather than through the need's text. That lens asks what the next reader finds when a holder is gone: a close-out that loses its handoff strands the next coordinator, and one that can launch twice creates competing claim authority. An atomic, owned request consumed once was chosen over treating every session-end event as a relaunch because clear and resume do not end the coordinator and a failed launch must remain recoverable. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name an orderly handover of the coordinating role as part of a drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-301
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: session_prompt extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. request_relaunch refuses inside a blackout window and otherwise accepts only the lease holder and an existing handoff, then writes a same-session request atomically. The hook's on_blackout SessionEnd dispatch calls blackout_session_end to cancel a pending request and record blackout inside a window; otherwise session_end acts only for a true exit by that holder in its declared repository, rejects a foreign request, and atomically renames one request to acquire it and holds the store lock from that acquisition until the launch is confirmed or the request restored, so restoration never waits on the lock. It writes a fresh successor token into the lease and the handoff prompt to a file, invokes the platform launcher detached in the declared root with that file and token, and on any failure before launch confirmation restores the request and removes the successor token and the prompt file. launch_command selects the Windows or POSIX launcher; launch_detached waits through its grace period and confirms only a still-running launcher or one that exits zero. Each launcher enters the declared root, resolves one interpreter by running its virtual-environment candidate followed by the candidates from its dev-setup's ordinary runtime search, and takes the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it exits nonzero with guidance when none resolves, runs window_check on that interpreter, exports the token as PT_COORDINATOR_TAKE for the successor's SessionStart take, and starts claude with the prompt as one argument: the POSIX one in a new terminal, the Windows one through exec_claude, which invokes claude with the extracted prompt. main dispatches the guard operations; reopening a session with the context latch still set needs the operator recorded clear.
  - after: The session-prompt reading extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. The relaunch request refuses inside a blackout window and otherwise accepts only the lease holder and an existing handoff, then writes a same-session request atomically. Inside a blackout window the hook's session-end handling cancels a pending request and records blackout; otherwise the session-end handling acts only for a true exit by that holder in its declared repository, rejects a foreign request, and atomically renames one request to acquire it and holds the store lock from that acquisition until the launch is confirmed or the request restored, so restoration never waits on the lock. It writes a fresh successor token into the lease and the handoff prompt to a file, invokes the platform launcher detached in the declared root with that file and token, and on any failure before launch confirmation restores the request and removes the successor token and the prompt file. The launch selects the Windows or POSIX launcher, waits through its grace period and confirms only a still-running launcher or one that exits zero. Each launcher enters the declared root, resolves one interpreter by running its virtual-environment candidate followed by the candidates from its development setup's ordinary runtime search, and takes the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it exits nonzero with guidance when none resolves, runs the guard's blackout window check on that interpreter, starting nothing when it refuses, exports the token in the successor-token variable for the successor's session-start take, and starts the agent command-line tool with the prompt as one argument: the POSIX one in a new terminal, the Windows one through the guard's prompt-file step, which invokes the agent command-line tool with the extracted prompt. The guard's command line dispatches the guard operations; reopening a session with the context latch still set needs the operator recorded clear. Run from the command line, the lease take, release, clear, relaunch request, hand-back and status operations exit 0 when they succeed, and one that refuses prints its reason on standard error after the guard's own prefix and exits 1; a usage error exits 2, and the hook operation exits 0 whatever it reads.

## SR-231 — Coordinator adjudication enters from a claimed lane and accepts only its current verdict

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordinator adjudication entry point shall, regardless of the declared adjudicator retention dial, run only from a claimed lane worktree and read success only from a verdict that its own call wrote within that call's deadline. From a claimed lane, it takes only an act an accepted current verdict authorizes. The verdict, reservation, and deadline obligations stated first apply to the judging turn; the act turn's success is the completion contract stated in the acceptance criteria.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that a configured agent progress without a human curating what comes next; it does not state the entry boundary for an adjudication the coordinator starts, so this obligation arrives through two lenses. The unattended-operations lens listens for a run whose telemetry lands on trunk instead of the claimed work it judges, leaving a later reader unable to recover its work item context. The integrity lens listens for stale evidence being accepted as this call's success: a verdict left by an earlier or overdue call can make an unsuccessful current call look valid. A lane-local call that owns a fresh verdict reservation was chosen over trusting a caller-supplied path or a shared trunk record, because both leave the evidence's author and time ambiguous. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name attributable, current adjudication evidence as part of unattended progress; until it does, this row is derived and says so. An act is authorized only by an accepted, committed verdict, so it cannot substitute later work for the judgement that authorized it.


### LLR LLR-306
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: ENTRY. coordinator_adjudicate runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. It also refuses an unusable route, invalid account, registry error, or unreadable or undecodable brief through that interface; its sign-in reading identifies the adjudicator entry’s account. As its last pre-launch checks it creates the verdict path's missing parent directories and then the path itself, empty and exclusively, and creates its requested-kinds binding beside it exclusively, so an existing or concurrently reserved path or binding, or one whose parent cannot be created, also refuses; its exclusive binding is made through the shared bind_pending request binder; a pre-launch refusal removes only the reservation and binding this call created and leaves any directory it created. Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an ADJUDICATE call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit, and prints the IF-285 reading. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.

Otherwise it composes the shared request, plans the keep, launches and records an adjudication call with coordinator attribution, then validates the verdict and records accepted only when that validation and a successful call - exit 0, in deadline, and no reported error result - both hold, otherwise failed, and commits that binding in its own telemetry commit. It prints the call's retention, a fresh session, a retained session minted or one resumed, and the call's exit, then, for a successful call, the verdict it wrote with its validity or that it wrote none, and, for a failed call, that its verdict is not read. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it prints the call's retention and exit, then the acts still owed after the call and the review records the call changed, or that the act is complete; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.
- **Rationale**
  - before: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness, while IF-283, IF-284 and IF-285 each own the command contract they expose; copying those contracts here was rejected because two homes for the same route or exit condition would drift.
  - after: This one entry point serves SR-227's coordinator-route and sign-in obligations as well as SR-231's lane-local admission and evidence-freshness boundary: a call outside its claimed lane could commit telemetry on trunk, and a path not exclusively reserved for this call could carry an earlier call's verdict. The entry point therefore owns admission and evidence freshness. The command's interfaces state its form, its default route and its exit codes, and this row states the behaviour a caller observes across them: the refusals, the recorded outcome and the reading it prints.

## SR-232 — Combined adjudication preserves each requested judgement

> **Requirement.** Where one lane checkpoint requests several declared adjudication kinds, the delivered adjudication content shall present and validate one sitting that preserves each kind's own scope, evidence and decision grammar.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks for unattended progress but does not require one sitting to carry several judgements. The unattended-operations lens catches a passing checkpoint where a requested kind was never judged; the integrity-recoverability lens catches a partial write where one kind acts while another section refuses.  A composed sitting was chosen so each judgement remains independently checkable while sharing its checkpoint. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name bounded, attributable checkpoint adjudication; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

## SR-234 — Contested or repeated review findings receive a bounded adjudication

> **Requirement.** Where a review finding is contested by the builder or coordinator, or recurs for a third review round, the delivered adjudication content shall preserve each requested finding, the position held on it and its relevant change scope, accept only a verdict that rules every requested finding exactly once as a fix, a successor row, a reasoned dismissal in a declared class, or an escalation to the operator, and direct the dispute sitting to rule out a successor row for a fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when-gap finding.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-006 asks an unattended run to report failures clearly and surface to an operator when it cannot proceed, but it does not state who ends a contested or repeated review finding. The unattended-operations lens listens for an unbounded review loop in which the coordinator sends the same finding back to the builder without a bounded, attributable decision. A complete adjudication was chosen so an independent call can settle each requested finding without silently adding, dropping or defaulting one. Fed back to the need: SN-006's acceptance could state that contested review findings receive a bounded ruling; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-314
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: kitlib.dispute accepts one TOML findings file with only a non-empty `range` and a non-empty `[[finding]]` list. Each finding has exactly a unique letter-led id, held_by `builder` or `coordinator`, and non-empty finding and position text, which the reader keeps verbatim apart from every leading and trailing newline character; spaces and interior lines are kept. request_line writes the composed request, and requested_ids reads its first DISPUTE findings line. The composed brief's findings-file line names the findings file the brief was composed over; its first such line supplies the path, and no line or an empty one supplies none. parse reads RULING lines one physical line at a time and accepts only one FIX, SUCCESSOR, DISMISS, or ESCALATE ruling with a reason for every requested id; DISMISS additionally takes only out-of-scope, refuted, or not-worth-cost and a reason. Missing, duplicate, unrequested, malformed, or incomplete content refuses; no value is defaulted. SUCCESSOR takes a reason and no reason class; an unknown ruling refusal names FIX, SUCCESSOR, DISMISS, and ESCALATE in that order.
  - after: The shared dispute grammar accepts one TOML findings file holding only a non-empty range and a non-empty list of findings. Each finding has exactly a unique letter-led id, the party holding its position, builder or coordinator, and non-empty finding and position text, which the reader keeps verbatim apart from every leading and trailing newline character; spaces and interior lines are kept. The grammar writes the composed request, and the requested ids are read from a brief's first DISPUTE findings line. The composed brief's findings-file line names the findings file the brief was composed over; its first such line supplies the path, and no line or an empty one supplies none. The verdict reader reads RULING lines one physical line at a time and accepts only one FIX, SUCCESSOR, DISMISS, or ESCALATE ruling with a reason for every requested id; DISMISS additionally takes only out-of-scope, refuted, or not-worth-cost and a reason. Missing, duplicate, unrequested, malformed, or incomplete content refuses; no value is defaulted. SUCCESSOR takes a reason and no reason class; an unknown ruling refusal names FIX, SUCCESSOR, DISMISS, and ESCALATE in that order.

## SR-235 — Attended independent review verdicts are recorded or refused

> **Requirement.** When an attended launcher submits an independent review verdict for a work item's lane, the delivered review content shall record a verdict bound to the reviewed commit in the review-round record form the delivered verdict reporting reads, or refuse a malformed or unbound verdict without a record.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-024 requires an independent critical eye and assigns the attended reviewer to the operator, but does not require that verdict to survive as review history. The integrity-and-recoverability lens listens for a durable artifact updated without an all-or-nothing guarantee: accepting an unbound or malformed verdict can associate the wrong judgement with a lane, while dropping a recorded verdict leaves the next reader with no record of the independent review. A validated record-or-refusal boundary was chosen over treating submitted text as history because the latter cannot distinguish a verdict for the reviewed commit from unrelated text. Fed back to the need: SN-024's acceptance could name a durable attended-review record; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-313
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. render_review strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; render_critique strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. review_refusal accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form `VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>`, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. round_path chooses the next ordinal lane round filename, file_review writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and lane_commits resolves commits on a branch and requires the base to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A first plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when alone, opens with that commit, and refuses a missing rubric, plan or specification, a missing or beyond delta, a coverage failure, or a third round. A second plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when and the first critique's finding identifiers, never their text, uses convergence stance, and refuses a revision that leaves a first-critique finding neither covered nor excluded. A single-plan brief strictly fills the shipped single-plan template with the specification, ordered Done-when clauses, required registry references, and one planner output path; a revision additionally labels the committed plan and latest filed critique findings as claims under judgement. It refuses a missing specification, Done-when, plan or critique, or an unfilled slot. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. At the cap, a dispute findings file is written only from the committed second critique, giving each blocking and major finding the identifier of its position among all critique findings, its text verbatim, and a declared position; it refuses another identifier set or an existing file. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. A plan critique ties every severity class to a normal-use-reachable defect: a fail-open, data-loss, or security path counts only when a supported workflow or the kit's own routes reach it, and a rare or contrived path is MINOR at most or dismissed. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar.
  - after: A review round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. The review render strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; the critique render strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. Filing accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. A filed round takes the lane's next ordinal round file name, filing writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and the lane's commits are resolved on a branch, its base required to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar. ## SR-237 — Blackout pauses new model work and resumes it after the window

> **Requirement.** When a declared blackout window is armed, the delivered coordination and unattended operation shall pause new model work until the window ends, preserving each claimed lane at its committed pause point and starting no model work inside the window except an adjudication needed to bring an active lane to that point, then resume eligible work after the window without retaining a session whose prior use predates that window's end.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 requires a declared pause to stop claiming and drain work already in flight without double assignment. The unattended-operations lens identifies a high-usage interval as a pause that must not discard lane state, while the integrity lens identifies a stale retained transcript as an unsafe substitute for a fresh resume. Keeping the active claim and committed evidence was chosen over a second held state because it preserves the recovery model.


### LLR LLR-316
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: blackout_at is the sole reader of the declared blackout value. At a supplied or current UTC instant it returns the declared value, whether the instant is inside an enabled interval, that interval's exclusive end, and the most recent interval end at or before the instant. It treats absent, malformed and equal endpoints as disabled, starts intervals only on weekdays, and assigns a midnight-wrapping interval to its start weekday.
  - after: The blackout reading is the sole reader of the declared blackout value. At a supplied or current UTC instant it returns the declared value, whether the instant is inside an enabled interval, that interval's exclusive end, and the most recent interval end at or before the instant. It treats absent, malformed and equal endpoints as disabled, starts intervals only on weekdays, and assigns a midnight-wrapping interval to its start weekday.

## SR-239 — Where the reviewer retention dial is on, loop review routes retain only their scoped reviewer sessions

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop review content shall, if the reviewer setting is malformed, or names a persisted final reviewer while retention is off, read it as retention off and a fresh final reviewer; otherwise, where the declared reviewer retention dial is above zero, resume a reviewer only across iteration rounds for one lane, claim and review phase, keyed by those values and the iteration stage; read an absent, unreadable, malformed, misfiled or differently keyed retained record as no session and start fresh; never keep a reviewer session warm; retire that iteration session when the lane's full-lane round is drawn; run that round fresh, or, where the declared final-reviewer setting selects persistence, on one retained final reviewer per lane and phase that never resumes an iteration session; treat a change to the template a retained reviewer's brief is built from as a change of its governing inputs; and record session-mode and keep-scope as audit-only columns.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement. The Performance, Unattended Operations and Integrity Recoverability lenses identify the operating cost, unattended judgement degradation, and shared-state corruption risk of repeatedly minting reviewer sessions. Repeated fresh reviewer rounds can exhaust the available plan, while scoped identity prevents one lane or review stage from inheriting another transcript. Coordinator-written review-scope and session-generation records, not reviewer-authored text, establish final-review provenance; session-mode and keep-scope remain audit-only. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name bounded operating cost per reviewer judgement as part of what lets a run proceed with no human curating it; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

## SR-240 — Commit-floor activation before agent routes

> **Requirement.** Where a checkout's commit floor is inactive, the delivered agent routes shall refuse to start and name the applicable repair that activates the floor.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement through the SECURITY lens: an agent route can create a governed commit, and an inactive commit floor leaves that action without the enforcement SN-005 expects. Start-time refusal was selected over a configurable bypass so the route cannot grant itself the authority the floor is meant to enforce.


### LLR LLR-340
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: For a checkout root, the commit-floor activation contract returns no refusal only when the resolved hooks directory is the checkout's or primary checkout's .githooks directory and both commit hooks can run; otherwise it returns a refusal naming the detected cause and its repair: activation for an unsuitable hooks path or unavailable Git answer, restoration for a missing hook, or executable-mode repair for an unrunnable hook. Each route applies that result before it creates route-specific work, and a session launch distinguishes this refusal from a blackout refusal.
  - after: For a checkout root, the commit-floor activation contract returns no refusal only when the resolved hooks directory is the hooks directory at the top of the checkout or of the primary checkout and both commit hooks can run; otherwise it returns a refusal naming the detected cause and its repair: activation for an unsuitable hooks path or unavailable Git answer, restoration for a missing hook, or executable-mode repair for an unrunnable hook. Each route applies that result before it creates route-specific work, and a session launch distinguishes this refusal from a blackout refusal.

## SR-241 — Review briefs render only over a matching verified passed-checks record

> **Requirement.** When an attended launcher requests a review brief for a lane commit, the delivered review content shall render it only when that commit, or a commit beneath it from which only record changes or no changes separate it, carries a verified record that the declared commit checks and the test set derived from the work item's requirements passed on its tree; otherwise it refuses without a brief and names the cause and the step that makes the record.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-004 and SN-008 require trustworthy gate outcomes, but do not say that a metered independent-review session must be withheld when its requested tree is already known not to meet its declared commit checks. The performance lens listens for operating cost left unassessed: a review over such a tree spends the scarce review session on a failure the process can name before dispatch, while a record-only change must not require the same checks again. Trusting the verified record was chosen over running the checks during rendering because rendering then stays a bounded reader of a prior result rather than a second executor with a potentially different result. The coordinator and unattended review routes decompose this shared boundary separately. Fed back to the needs: their acceptance could state that a review begins only from a verified commit-check result.

## SR-243 — Coordinator calls record sessions without committing outside claimed work

> **Requirement.** When the coordinator's tooling launches a model session, the delivered coordinator call capability shall record the session in the usage record, record its log without committing to the shared line of development when it starts outside claimed work, refuse an unsafe resumed authoring carrier before launch, judge the call's changes against its starting state, and provide the sole launch route for author, critic and REVIEW-A calls.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 requires attributable routing among model families, and SR-222 requires a usage record for sessions the delivered loop launches, but neither reaches a coordinator-started session or states where its record may be committed. The unattended-operations lens identifies a partial or missing call record that leaves unattended progress unrecoverable, while the integrity-and-recoverability lens identifies telemetry committed to unrelated shared work as evidence with ambiguous provenance. Recording every coordinator session through the same usage record while retaining an out-of-work log without a shared commit was chosen over leaving coordinator sessions unrecorded or treating every checkout as a commit authority. Fed back to the need: its acceptance could state that every coordinator-started model session has attributable usage and a recoverable record without changing unrelated shared work. SR-231's rationale supplies the lane-local provenance lens; its adjudication-only boundary remains unchanged.

## SR-244 — Transcript-derived usage records include coordinated nested agents

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordination capability shall record once each transcript-bearing model session that coordination completion ingests for coordinated work, including every session an agent spawns at any nesting depth, with its identity and transcript-derived usage; record no already-recorded message, duplicate no service record, grant its record no gate authority, include no transcript text, refuse an unknown source shape, and direct completion to ingest the current session and a named prior-session tail.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement through the PERFORMANCE lens: SN-026 makes model-family routing a configurable operating choice, but does not require the cost of coordinated sessions outside the launcher result to be visible. The lens identifies an incomplete cost reading when nested work is omitted or a repeated or resumed read inflates it. Recorded probes establish that a resume appends to its existing transcript, that a Claude result excludes sub-agent usage, and that arbitrary nesting occurs; a sub-agent's final hand-back often has no stop record and is complete once the session's transcripts show the agent finished. The direct-child fan-out re-stamp is intentional: the independently observable reader, identity, contribution, recovery, duplication, shape, source-selection, record, CLI, and completion decisions must not be merged. Fed back to the need: its acceptance could require the operator to see complete, non-duplicated usage for coordinated work across the configured families.
